  "abstract_verbatim": "We prove that the unique ground state of a uniformly gapped nearest-neighbor Hamiltonian on an $`L\\times L`$ square lattice admits a projected entangled-pair state approximation with bond dimension polynomial in <i>L</i> and global vector error at most <i>L</i><sup>−1</sup> after normalization. <a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that the unique ground state of a uniformly gapped nearest-neighbor Hamiltonian on an $`L\times L`$ square lattice admits a projected entangled-pair state approximation with bond dimension polynomial in <i>L</i> and global vector error at most <i>L</i><sup>−1</sup> after normalization. Only the gap of the full Hamiltonian is assumed. The result is an existence theorem, with constants uniform over Hamiltonians of fixed local dimension, interaction strength, and gap.

- **claim-polynomial-original-grid-peps** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Fix an integer $q\ge2$ and constants $J,\Delta>0$. There are constants $C,c>0$, depending only on $q,J,\Delta$, with the following property. For every $L\ge2$, consider any Hermitian Hamiltonian $$\label{eq:model} H=\sum_{v\in\Lambda_L}h_v+\sum_{e\in E(\Lambda_L)}h_e, \qquad \lVert h_v\rVert,\lVert h_e\rVert\le J,$$ where each summand is supported on its designated site or edge. Suppose that $H$ has a unique normalized ground vector $\Omega$, up to phase, with ground energy $E_0$, and $$\label{eq:global-gap} H-E_0I\ge\Delta\bigl(I-\|\Omega\rangle\langle\Omega\|\bigr).$$ Then there is a nonzero PEPS $\Phi$ on the same grid with maximum bond dimension at most $CL^c$ such that $$\label{eq:target-error} \min_{\theta\in\mathbb R} \left\\|\frac{\Phi}{\lVert \Phi\rVert}-e^{i\theta}\Omega\right\\| \le L^{-1}.$$
  - Explanation: A fixed local dimension, interaction bound, and full-system spectral gap give a uniform polynomial bond bound for a normalized global-vector PEPS approximation on the original open square. Tensor discovery and contraction efficiency are not conclusions.
- **claim-uniform-cut-entropy-area-law** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-area-law)): Under <a href="#eq:model" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:model">[eq:model]</a>–<a href="#eq:global-gap" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:global-gap">[eq:global-gap]</a>, there is $C_A=C_A(q,J,\Delta)$ such that every $A\subseteq\Lambda$ satisfies $$S_\Omega(A)\le C_A\|\partial_\Lambda A\|, \qquad \partial_\Lambda A=\{\{x,y\}\in E(\Lambda):x\in A, \ y\notin A\}.$$ The same constant works for every $L$ and every Hamiltonian in the class.
  - Explanation: The imported entropy bound applies to every subsystem of the original ground state with the same boundary constant. Auxiliary regions do not require independently gapped Hamiltonians.
- **claim-fidelity-purification-information** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-fidelity)): For normalized finite-dimensional states, $$\label{eq:fidelity-information} F(\rho,\sigma)\ge e^{-D(\rho\Vert\sigma)/2}, \qquad D(\rho_{AC}\Vert\rho_A\otimes\rho_C)=I(A:C),$$ Moreover, after enlarging the purifying space if necessary, an isometry on that space realizes overlap $F(\rho,\sigma)$ between purifications. In particular a mutual-information bound $\delta$ gives purifications of the joint state and product state at vector distance at most $\sqrt\delta$, after phase choice, since $2(1-e^{-\delta/2})\le\delta$.
  - Explanation: Relative entropy bounds root fidelity from below, and an enlarged purifying space realizes the optimal overlap by an isometry. Small mutual information therefore permits nearby joint and product purifications.
- **claim-coarse-rectangular-collar-information** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-coarse)): Fix an integer $N_0\ge1$ and a constant $A_0\ge1$. There are $\beta<1$, $C<\infty$, and $u_0<\infty$ such that the following holds. Let $u\ge u_0$ and let $T$ be the full ambient lattice sample of a union of at most $N_0$ axis-parallel rectangles, with $\mathop{\mathrm{diam}}_\infty T\le A_0u$. For every physical set $E\subseteq\Lambda$ satisfying $$\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}_\infty(E,T)>u^\beta,$$ one has $$I_\Omega(\Lambda\cap T:E)\le C.$$ The same statement holds for any subset of $\Lambda\cap T$ in its first argument, for rectangles with consistently assigned open or closed sides, and after a common translation of the lattice coordinates.
  - Explanation: A bounded rectangular union has uniformly bounded information with a physical set outside a sublinear collar. Discarding part of the template and consistent boundary conventions preserve the conclusion.
- **claim-cell-conditional-information** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-cell-cmi)): Fix $c>0$. Tile the plane by an axis-parallel grid of squares of side $s\ge1$. Let $G$ be the physical sample of one cell, let $B$ be the physical sample of any union of other cells, and let $C\subseteq\Lambda\setminus(B\cup G)$ satisfy $$\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}_\infty(C,\overline{G}_{\rm amb})\ge cs,$$ where $\overline{G}_{\rm amb}$ denotes the full closed ambient cell. There are fixed constants $C_1,K<\infty$ such that $$I_\Omega(G:C\mid B)\le C_1(1+\log s)^K. \label{eq:info-cell-cmi}$$ The constants are independent of which exterior cells comprise $B$ and of the shape of $C$ away from the cell.
  - Explanation: A cell has a polylogarithmic conditional-information cost against a separated exterior set, even when conditioning on an arbitrary union of other cells. The conditioning order is established by the proof rather than inferred from ordinary mutual information.
- **claim-global-gap-information-reset** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-reset)): For every fixed $p>0$ there is $C_p<\infty$ with the following property for all sufficiently large $L$. Let $R,C\subseteq\Lambda$ be disjoint, let $R_0\subseteq R$ and $C_0\subseteq C$, and suppose $$I_\Omega(R:C)\le b,\qquad b\ge0.$$ If $$\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}_\infty(R_0,\Lambda\setminus R)>w,\qquad \mathop{\mathrm{dist}}_\infty(C_0,\Lambda\setminus C)>w, \qquad w=C_p(1+b+\log L), \label{eq:info-reset-width}$$ then $$I_\Omega(R_0:C_0)\le L^{-p}.$$ Only the original full-system gap is assumed.
  - Explanation: After shrinking disjoint regions by a buffer proportional to their information cost and the logarithm of lattice size, the remaining cores have arbitrarily small inverse-polynomial mutual information. The spectral assumption remains the original full-system gap.
- **claim-angular-separation-information** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-angular)): Fix $a>0$, $A_0\ge1$, an integer $m_0\ge0$, and $p>0$. There is $D<\infty$ such that the following holds for all sufficiently large $L$. Let $\mathcal V$ be a set of at most $m_0$ marked points in the plane, let $X,E\subseteq\Lambda$ be disjoint, and let $$D\log L\le n\le A_0L, \qquad \mathop{\mathrm{diam}}_\infty X\le A_0n.$$ Put $s_{\min}=D\log L$ and $d_{\mathcal V}(x)=\min_{v\in\mathcal V}\|x-v\|_\infty$, with $d_{\varnothing}=+\infty$. Suppose that for every $x\in X$, $$\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}_\infty(x,E) \ge a\max\{s_{\min},\min(n,d_{\mathcal V}(x))\}. \label{eq:info-angular-separation}$$ Then $$I_\Omega(X:E)\le L^{-p}.$$ The constant $D$ and the scale threshold are uniform in the marked points, physical sets, and Hamiltonian.
  - Explanation: Tapered separation toward a bounded set of marked points suffices for inverse-polynomial mutual information, provided a logarithmic minimum clearance remains. Information is reset after each annular layer.
- **claim-adaptive-orthogonal-patch-constraint** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-patch)): Fix $B_0>0$ and $p>0$. There are constants $C,c>0$, depending only on $q,J,\Delta,B_0,p$, with the following property. For every $L\geq2$, center $z\in\mathbb R^2$, and $0<u\leq B_0\log L$, there is an orthogonal projection $P$, supported on $X(2u)$, such that $$\lVert (\mathop{\mathrm{id}}-P)\Omega\rVert\leq L^{-p}.$$ There are radii $u\leq r_1\leq\cdots\leq r_m\leq2u$, sets $X_j=X(r_j)$, and unit vectors $v_{j\ell}\in\mathcal H_{X_j}$, $1\leq\ell\leq d_j$, for which $$P=\sum_{j=1}^m\sum_{\ell=1}^{d_j} \lvert v_{j\ell}\rangle\langle v_{j\ell}\rvert_{X_j} \otimes\mathop{\mathrm{id}}_{\Lambda\setminus X_j}, \qquad \sum_{j=1}^m d_j\leq C L^c. \label{eq:patch-cylinder-projection}$$ All the summands in <a href="#eq:patch-cylinder-projection" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:patch-cylinder-projection">[eq:patch-cylinder-projection]</a> have pairwise orthogonal ranges as operators on $\mathcal H_\Lambda$. No bound on the internal tensor complexity of the individual $v_{j\ell}$ is asserted.
  - Explanation: An accurate patch projection is expressed as mutually orthogonal cylinders at nested, potentially different radii, with polynomial total inside rank. This does not give a truncation at a single prescribed boundary or control the internal tensor complexity of the inside vectors.
- **claim-normalized-pair-effect-elimination** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-effects)): Let $G$ be a contraction with an expansion $G=\sum_{\xi}c_{\xi}M_{\xi}$ into $K$ allowed monomials on a fixed set of parties. Suppose each monomial has at most $r$ pair effects. For every integer $m\ge1$ there is a gate $\widetilde G_m$, with additional garbage registers, and a fixed normalized garbage vector $\Gamma_m$, such that $$\lVert \widetilde G_m-G\otimes\|\Gamma_m\rangle\rVert \le \frac{r}{\sqrt m}\sum_{\xi}\|c_{\xi}\|. \label{eq:compression-effect-error}$$ The gate $\widetilde G_m$ has an expansion using only local contractions and normalized pair sources. It has at most $K m^r$ monomials, and their absolute coefficient sum is at most $\sum_{\xi}\|c_{\xi}\|$. Its additional registers are owned by the original participating parties. All sources on the same pair of parties may be combined into one normalized pair source.
  - Explanation: Permutation averages replace pair effects by prepared pair sources with a common branch-independent garbage output. The operator error decreases with the stack length while the absolute coefficient sum does not increase.
- **claim-distributed-density-compression** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-compression)): Consider a family of distributed constructions indexed by $L\ge2$ with the following uniform properties.* 1. *The number of parties and gates is uniformly polynomial. The initial vector is a tensor product of normalized private vectors, one at each party, and every gate is a contraction.* 2. *There is a constant $b$ such that each nonprivate gate involves at most $b$ parties, and each party participates in at most $b$ nonprivate gates over the entire construction.* 3. *Each nonprivate gate is a sum of uniformly polynomially many allowed monomials with uniformly polynomially bounded scalar coefficients. There is a constant $r$ bounding the number of pair sources, pair effects, and polynomial-dimensional messages in each monomial. All constituent local maps are contractions.* 4. *At the end, the intended physical output space of each party has uniformly polynomial dimension. All other registers are traced out party by party.* *Write $\rho$ for the resulting physical output density operator; it is positive and may have trace less than one. For every fixed $p>0$, there is an operator tensor network $\sigma$ satisfying $$\lVert \sigma-\rho\rVert_1\le L^{-p}.$$ It has one node per party, a bounded number of incident virtual links at each node, and uniformly polynomial link dimensions. Every link joins two parties which participate in a common nonprivate gate of the original construction. The physical legs at a node are the ket and bra legs of that party’s intended output. The constants in these bounds depend only on $p$ and the fixed uniform data in the assumptions, including $b,r$ and the polynomial bounds, and not on private dimensions or source Schmidt ranks.* *The same conclusion holds if the gate expansions in the third assumption are available to every fixed inverse-polynomial operator-norm accuracy, with uniform polynomial bounds at each fixed accuracy.
  - Explanation: Polynomially many contraction gates with bounded lifetime participation and polynomial resource expansions admit an absolute trace-norm approximation by an operator tensor network with polynomial links. Private dimensions and resource Schmidt ranks do not enter the bound, and the compressed operator need not be positive.
- **claim-whole-group-tensor-truncation** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-group-tensor)): Let a finite graph carry at each vertex a Hilbert-space tensor of norm at most one. Contract its internal edges by the usual coordinate pairing, and suppose there are no edges from a vertex to itself. Group the open legs according to their original vertices. The resulting tensor $Z$ has norm at most one and has matrix nuclear norm at most one across every bipartition into whole open-leg groups.* *If there are $g$ nonempty open-leg groups, then, for every integer $k\ge1$, there are subspaces of dimension at most $k$ in the respective group spaces whose tensor-product projection sends $Z$ to a tensor $Z_k$ with $$\label{eq:group-truncation} \lVert Z-Z_k\rVert\le\frac{g}{\sqrt{k}}.$$ In orthonormal bases of those subspaces, $Z_k$ has at most $k^g$ product terms, with coefficients of absolute value at most one.
  - Explanation: Contracting normalized vertex tensors without self-edges controls the nuclear norm across whole original open-leg groups. Separate singular-subspace projections then yield a product expansion whose error is independent of wire dimensions.
- **claim-small-patch-compatible-rewrite** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-small-rewrite)): Consider old and new one-sheet frames. Only a bounded number of their holes are affected; all other holes, encodings, and tag owners agree. Suppose there are $m=O(1)$ additional square patches $(c_i,u_i)$, with $0<u_i\le B_0\log L$, satisfying the following conditions.* 1. *Every patch’s outer square avoids the physical outer footprints of the untouched holes. Affected old and new holes also avoid those untouched footprints.* 2. *The union of the patch inner squares contains every site whose raw owner changes and the entire physical outer footprints of every affected old or new hole.* 3. *In each patch’s outer square, sites outside $H^-(\mathcal F_{\rm old})$ have at most two old owners. Separately, sites outside $H^-(\mathcal F_{\rm new})$ have at most two new owners.* 4. *All raw owners on the involved patch and affected-hole footprints, and all affected tag owners, belong to a specified bounded list of parties.* *The patches may overlap, and an old affected hole may overlap new affected holes. There is a canonical contraction $M$ between the two layouts with $$\label{eq:rewrite-reference-error} \lVert M\Omega_{\mathcal F_{\rm old}}- \Omega_{\mathcal F_{\rm new}}\rVert \le(m+r_{\rm old})\varepsilon,$$ where $r_{\rm old}$ is the number of affected old holes. For every fixed $a>0$, $M$ admits a contractive approximation $M_a$ with $\lVert M_a-M\rVert\le L^{-a}$ and a polynomial sum expansion of the kind required by Theorem <a href="#thm:compression" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:compression">10</a>, using only the specified parties. In particular the rewrite is a bounded change with reference-vector error $O(L^{-20})$.
  - Explanation: A bounded patch cover implements a compatible change of raw ownership and hole encodings. Separate old-owner and new-owner tests ensure every remaining open tensor group belongs to at most a pair of parties, even when patches overlap.
- **claim-buffer-purification-splitting** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-splitting)): Let $T,U,E$ partition $\Lambda$, and let $b=I_\Omega(T:E)$. There are finite-dimensional spaces $\mathcal B_T$ and $\mathcal B_E$, an isometry $$V:\mathcal H_U\longrightarrow\mathcal B_T\otimes\mathcal B_E,$$ and normalized vectors $s\in\mathcal H_T\otimes\mathcal B_T$ and $s'\in\mathcal H_E\otimes\mathcal B_E$ such that $$\label{eq:frame-splitting} \lVert (\mathop{\mathrm{id}}_{TE}\otimes V)\Omega-s\otimes s'\rVert \le\sqrt{2(1-e^{-b/2})}\le\sqrt b.$$ There is no bound on the dimensions of the two new spaces. In particular, if $b\le L^{-60}$, the error is at most $L^{-30}$.
  - Explanation: Small mutual information permits an isometry on the separating buffer that makes the state close to a product of purifications. The enlarged buffer factors have no dimension bound.
- **claim-homogeneous-ownership-birth-death** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-birth)): In an encoded frame, let $T$ be a set of raw sites owned by $P_\circ$ and disjoint from every outer hole footprint. The proposed birth changes only the owner of $T$, from $P_\circ$ to $Q_\circ$. Keep every hole, its encoding, and its tag owner fixed. Define $$\label{eq:birth-partition} E=\{x:\text{the old raw owner of }x\ne P_\circ\} \cup H^+(\mathcal F),\qquad U=\Lambda\setminus(T\cup E).$$ If $I_\Omega(T:E)\le L^{-60}$, the birth is a bounded change involving only $P_\circ,Q_\circ$, with reference-vector error at most $L^{-30}$. The reversed death obeys the same bound whenever these ownership and information conditions hold in its surrounding frame, namely the frame *after* the death.
  - Explanation: A region outside every hole can change homogeneous owner using a private buffer isometry and a normalized pair resource. The reverse operation must satisfy the information condition in the frame after the death.
- **claim-whole-hole-two-sheet-exchange** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-exchange)): Consider two encoded frames, and a plane region $Y$. Suppose every hole’s entire outer square lies on one side of $\partial Y$. Inside $Y$, exchange the two raw ownership assignments and the two lists of encoded holes, including their tag owners; outside $Y$, keep both frames unchanged. Let $P_\circ$ be a party, and on the physical square put $$\begin{aligned} Z&=\{x:\text{the two old raw owners of }x \text{ are not both }P_\circ\} \cup H^+(\mathcal F_1)\cup H^+(\mathcal F_2), \label{eq:exchange-Z}\\ T&=Z\cap Y,\qquad E=Z\setminus Y,\qquad U=\Lambda\setminus Z.\label{eq:exchange-partition} \end{aligned}$$ If $I_\Omega(T:E)\le L^{-60}$, the exchange can be implemented by private contractions and register renaming, with reference-vector error at most $4L^{-30}$. It uses $P_\circ$ and the owners of the registers and tags being renamed. Thus, when these parties form a bounded list, it is a bounded change in the sense of Theorem <a href="#thm:compression" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:compression">10</a>.
  - Explanation: Exchanging sheet assignments on a region is possible through private identifications and a common-owner buffer correction. Whole outer hole squares and tags must remain on the same side of the exchange boundary.
- **claim-bounded-lifetime-dyadic-distribution** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-protocol)): For all sufficiently large $L$, there is a protocol with the following properties. All constants in this statement are uniform in the Hamiltonian and $L$.* 1. *There are $O(L^2)$ parties, one for each square in a padded dyadic hierarchy and at most a fixed number of additional parties for each repainting of a square. Initialization is a pure product across parties. One party initially holds the main copy of $\Omega$; all auxiliary copies are initialized privately at their designated parties.* 2. *The schedule has at most $C L^3$ changes. Each change is implemented by a fixed number of contraction gates involving at most a fixed number of parties and having the bipartite-resource expansions established in Lemmas <a href="#lem:small-rewrite" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:small-rewrite">14</a>, <a href="#lem:birth" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:birth">16</a>, and <a href="#lem:exchange" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:exchange">17</a>. The expansions can be made accurate to any fixed inverse power of $L$ in operator norm where an approximation is needed. Each party participates in at most a fixed number of gates involving other parties over its entire lifetime.* 3. *Every linked gate involves parties at the same or adjacent dyadic scales. Parties at a gate have addresses at distance $O(n)$ from one another, where $n$ is either endpoint scale; additional parties have the scale and address of the square to whose repainting they belong.* 4. *There is a designated reference vector at every stage, formed from the main encoded frame and the auxiliary encoded frames. All its factors have norm at most one. Each change takes its preceding reference vector within $C L^{-20}$ in vector norm of its following reference vector. At the end, the reference is $$\Omega\otimes\Xi, \qquad 1-C L^{3-60}\le \lVert \Xi\rVert\le1,$$ where $\Xi$ contains only registers to be discarded. Every original physical site is a raw register at its own unit-square party. No other party has a physical output, so every party’s physical output dimension is at most $q$.
  - Explanation: The dyadic schedule distributes the privately supplied ground vector to site parties while keeping lifetime communication bounded. Auxiliary sheets and their retained norms remain in the reference comparison until the final discard.
- **claim-band-angular-clearance** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-geometry-angular)): There is a fixed $a_0>0$ such that every unmodified elementary birth or death above has changed region $R$ of diameter $O(n)$ and surrounding guide $f$ satisfying $$\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}\bigl(y,\overline{\{f\ne P_\circ\}}\bigr) \ge a_0\min\{n,d_{\mathcal V}(y)\}, \qquad y\in\overline R. \label{eq:geometry-birth-clearance}$$ For an unmodified lens exchange, every point in the closure of the positions that are not common $C$ on its two before guides satisfies $$\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(y,\partial Y) \ge a_0\min\{n,d_{\mathcal V}(y)\}. \label{eq:geometry-exchange-clearance}$$ Here and throughout this section distances are ambient sup distances.
  - Explanation: The fixed band templates have positive conical clearance away from marked contact points. Identifying nominal labels removes obstructions and does not weaken the separation already established.
- **claim-nonzero-product-basis-column** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-columns)): Let $\Omega$ be a unit vector on a tensor product of finite-dimensional spaces. If an operator $\sigma$ satisfies $\lVert \sigma-\|\Omega\rangle\langle\Omega\|\rVert_1\le\eta<1$, then some product-basis column $v=\sigma\|z\rangle$ is nonzero and satisfies $$\min_\theta\left\\|\frac{v}{\lVert v\rVert}-e^{i\theta}\Omega\right\\| \le2\eta.$$ Fixing this column in an operator tensor network leaves all its virtual dimensions unchanged.
  - Explanation: An operator close to a normalized pure-state projector contains a nonzero product-basis column whose normalized vector is close to the target. Selecting that column preserves virtual dimensions and does not require positivity or a lower bound on the selected amplitude.
- **claim-original-grid-constant-congestion-routing** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-routing)): The links of the party network can be routed on the padded grid with constant edge congestion. After reflection into the genuine $L\times L$ grid, congestion is still constant and physical output coordinates are unchanged.
  - Explanation: Comparable-scale nearby party links admit horizontal-then-vertical routing with constant congestion. Reflection of the padded grid preserves physical outputs and the polynomial bond bound.

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-fidelity",
        "exact_quote": "For normalized finite-dimensional states, $$\\label{eq:fidelity-information}\n F(\\rho,\\sigma)\\ge e^{-D(\\rho\\Vert\\sigma)/2},\n \\qquad\n D(\\rho_{AC}\\Vert\\rho_A\\otimes\\rho_C)=I(A:C),$$ Moreover, after enlarging the purifying space if necessary, an isometry on that space realizes overlap $F(\\rho,\\sigma)$ between purifications.         "exact_quote": "Fix an integer $N_0\\ge1$ and a constant $A_0\\ge1$. There are $\\beta<1$, $C<\\infty$, and $u_0<\\infty$ such that the following holds.         "exact_quote": "Fix $c>0$. Tile the plane by an axis-parallel grid of squares of side $s\\ge1$.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-reset",
        "exact_quote": "For every fixed $p>0$ there is $C_p<\\infty$ with the following property for all sufficiently large $L$.         "exact_quote": "Fix $a>0$, $A_0\\ge1$, an integer $m_0\\ge0$, and $p>0$. There is $D<\\infty$ such that the following holds for all sufficiently large $L$.         "exact_quote": "Fix $B_0>0$ and $p>0$. There are constants $C,c>0$, depending only on $q,J,\\Delta,B_0,p$, with the following property. For every $L\\geq2$, center $z\\in\\mathbb R^2$, and $0<u\\leq B_0\\log L$, there is an orthogonal projection $P$, supported on $X(2u)$, such that $$\\lVert (\\mathop{\\mathrm{id}}-P)\\Omega\\rVert\\leq L^{-p}.$$ There are radii $u\\leq r_1\\leq\\cdots\\leq r_m\\leq2u$, sets $X_j=X(r_j)$, and unit vectors $v_{j\\ell}\\in\\mathcal H_{X_j}$, $1\\leq\\ell\\leq d_j$, for which $$P=\\sum_{j=1}^m\\sum_{\\ell=1}^{d_j}\n       \\lvert v_{j\\ell}\\rangle\\langle v_{j\\ell}\\rvert_{X_j}\n          \\otimes\\mathop{\\mathrm{id}}_{\\Lambda\\setminus X_j},\n \\qquad\n \\sum_{j=1}^m d_j\\leq C L^c.\n \\label{eq:patch-cylinder-projection}$$ All the summands in <a href=\"#eq:patch-cylinder-projection\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:patch-cylinder-projection\">[eq:patch-cylinder-projection]</a> have pairwise orthogonal ranges as operators on $\\mathcal H_\\Lambda$.         {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-patch",
          "exact_quote": "There are constants $C,c>0$, depending only on $q,J,\\Delta,B_0,p$, with the following property.",
          "source_id": "PM-265.2"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-patch",
          "exact_quote": "For every $L\\geq2$, center $z\\in\\mathbb R^2$, and $0<u\\leq B_0\\log L$, there is an orthogonal projection $P$, supported on $X(2u)$, such that $$\\lVert (\\mathop{\\mathrm{id}}-P)\\Omega\\rVert\\leq L^{-p}.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-265.2"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-patch",
          "exact_quote": "There are radii $u\\leq r_1\\leq\\cdots\\leq r_m\\leq2u$, sets $X_j=X(r_j)$, and unit vectors $v_{j\\ell}\\in\\mathcal H_{X_j}$, $1\\leq\\ell\\leq d_j$, for which $$P=\\sum_{j=1}^m\\sum_{\\ell=1}^{d_j}\n       \\lvert v_{j\\ell}\\rangle\\langle v_{j\\ell}\\rvert_{X_j}\n          \\otimes\\mathop{\\mathrm{id}}_{\\Lambda\\setminus X_j},\n \\qquad\n \\sum_{j=1}^m d_j\\leq C L^c.\n \\label{eq:patch-cylinder-projection}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-265.2"
        }
      ],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-prop-patch"
    },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-effects",
        "exact_quote": "Let $G$ be a contraction with an expansion $G=\\sum_{\\xi}c_{\\xi}M_{\\xi}$ into $K$ allowed monomials on a fixed set of parties. Suppose each monomial has at most $r$ pair effects. For every integer $m\\ge1$ there is a gate $\\widetilde G_m$, with additional garbage registers, and a fixed normalized garbage vector $\\Gamma_m$, such that $$\\lVert \\widetilde G_m-G\\otimes|\\Gamma_m\\rangle\\rVert\n \\le \\frac{r}{\\sqrt m}\\sum_{\\xi}|c_{\\xi}|.\n \\label{eq:compression-effect-error}$$ The gate $\\widetilde G_m$ has an expansion using only local contractions and normalized pair sources. It has at most $K m^r$ monomials, and their absolute coefficient sum is at most $\\sum_{\\xi}|c_{\\xi}|$. Its additional registers are owned by the original participating parties. All sources on the same pair of parties may be combined into one normalized pair source.",
        "source_id": "PM-265.2"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-compression",
        "exact_quote": "Consider a family of distributed constructions indexed by $L\\ge2$ with the following uniform properties.*\n\n1.  *The number of parties and gates is uniformly polynomial. The initial vector is a tensor product of normalized private vectors, one at each party, and every gate is a contraction.*\n\n2.  *There is a constant $b$ such that each nonprivate gate involves at most $b$ parties, and each party participates in at most $b$ nonprivate gates over the entire construction.*\n\n3.  *Each nonprivate gate is a sum of uniformly polynomially many allowed monomials with uniformly polynomially bounded scalar coefficients. There is a constant $r$ bounding the number of pair sources, pair effects, and polynomial-dimensional messages in each monomial. All constituent local maps are contractions.*\n\n4.  *At the end, the intended physical output space of each party has uniformly polynomial dimension. All other registers are traced out party by party.*\n\n*Write $\\rho$ for the resulting physical output density operator; it is positive and may have trace less than one. For every fixed $p>0$, there is an operator tensor network $\\sigma$ satisfying $$\\lVert \\sigma-\\rho\\rVert_1\\le L^{-p}.$$ It has one node per party, a bounded number of incident virtual links at each node, and uniformly polynomial link dimensions. Every link joins two parties which participate in a common nonprivate gate of the original construction. The physical legs at a node are the ket and bra legs of that party’s intended output. The constants in these bounds depend only on $p$ and the fixed uniform data in the assumptions, including $b,r$ and the polynomial bounds, and not on private dimensions or source Schmidt ranks.*\n\n*The same conclusion holds if the gate expansions in the third assumption are available to every fixed inverse-polynomial operator-norm accuracy, with uniform polynomial bounds at each fixed accuracy.",
        "source_id": "PM-265.2"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-group-tensor",
        "exact_quote": "Let a finite graph carry at each vertex a Hilbert-space tensor of norm at most one. Contract its internal edges by the usual coordinate pairing, and suppose there are no edges from a vertex to itself. Group the open legs according to their original vertices. The resulting tensor $Z$ has norm at most one and has matrix nuclear norm at most one across every bipartition into whole open-leg groups.*\n\n\n*If there are $g$ nonempty open-leg groups, then, for every integer $k\\ge1$, there are subspaces of dimension at most $k$ in the respective group spaces whose tensor-product projection sends $Z$ to a tensor $Z_k$ with $$\\label{eq:group-truncation}\n \\lVert Z-Z_k\\rVert\\le\\frac{g}{\\sqrt{k}}.$$ In orthonormal bases of those subspaces, $Z_k$ has at most $k^g$ product terms, with coefficients of absolute value at most one.",
        "source_id": "PM-265.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-group-tensor",
          "exact_quote": "Let a finite graph carry at each vertex a Hilbert-space tensor of norm at most one.           "source_id": "PM-265.2"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-small-rewrite",
        "exact_quote": "Consider old and new one-sheet frames. Only a bounded number of their holes are affected; all other holes, encodings, and tag owners agree. Suppose there are $m=O(1)$ additional square patches $(c_i,u_i)$, with $0<u_i\\le B_0\\log L$, satisfying the following conditions.*\n\n1.  *Every patch’s outer square avoids the physical outer footprints of the untouched holes. Affected old and new holes also avoid those untouched footprints.*\n\n2.  *The union of the patch inner squares contains every site whose raw owner changes and the entire physical outer footprints of every affected old or new hole.*\n\n3.  *In each patch’s outer square, sites outside $H^-(\\mathcal F_{\\rm old})$ have at most two old owners. Separately, sites outside $H^-(\\mathcal F_{\\rm new})$ have at most two new owners.*\n\n4.  *All raw owners on the involved patch and affected-hole footprints, and all affected tag owners, belong to a specified bounded list of parties.*\n\n\n*The patches may overlap, and an old affected hole may overlap new affected holes. There is a canonical contraction $M$ between the two layouts with $$\\label{eq:rewrite-reference-error}\n \\lVert M\\Omega_{\\mathcal F_{\\rm old}}-\n                   \\Omega_{\\mathcal F_{\\rm new}}\\rVert\n       \\le(m+r_{\\rm old})\\varepsilon,$$ where $r_{\\rm old}$ is the number of affected old holes. For every fixed $a>0$, $M$ admits a contractive approximation $M_a$ with $\\lVert M_a-M\\rVert\\le L^{-a}$ and a polynomial sum expansion of the kind required by Theorem <a href=\"#thm:compression\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:compression\">10</a>, using only the specified parties. In particular the rewrite is a bounded change with reference-vector error $O(L^{-20})$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-splitting",
        "exact_quote": "Let $T,U,E$ partition $\\Lambda$, and let $b=I_\\Omega(T:E)$. There are finite-dimensional spaces $\\mathcal B_T$ and $\\mathcal B_E$, an isometry $$V:\\mathcal H_U\\longrightarrow\\mathcal B_T\\otimes\\mathcal B_E,$$ and normalized vectors $s\\in\\mathcal H_T\\otimes\\mathcal B_T$ and $s'\\in\\mathcal H_E\\otimes\\mathcal B_E$ such that $$\\label{eq:frame-splitting}\n \\lVert (\\mathop{\\mathrm{id}}_{TE}\\otimes V)\\Omega-s\\otimes s'\\rVert\n       \\le\\sqrt{2(1-e^{-b/2})}\\le\\sqrt b.$$ There is no bound on the dimensions of the two new spaces. In particular, if $b\\le L^{-60}$, the error is at most $L^{-30}$.",
        "source_id": "PM-265.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-splitting",
          "exact_quote": "Let $T,U,E$ partition $\\Lambda$, and let $b=I_\\Omega(T:E)$. There are finite-dimensional spaces $\\mathcal B_T$ and $\\mathcal B_E$, an isometry $$V:\\mathcal H_U\\longrightarrow\\mathcal B_T\\otimes\\mathcal B_E,$$ and normalized vectors $s\\in\\mathcal H_T\\otimes\\mathcal B_T$ and $s'\\in\\mathcal H_E\\otimes\\mathcal B_E$ such that $$\\label{eq:frame-splitting}\n \\lVert (\\mathop{\\mathrm{id}}_{TE}\\otimes V)\\Omega-s\\otimes s'\\rVert\n       \\le\\sqrt{2(1-e^{-b/2})}\\le\\sqrt b.$$ There is no bound on the dimensions of the two new spaces. In particular, if $b\\le L^{-60}$, the error is at most $L^{-30}$.",
          "source_id": "PM-265.2"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-birth",
        "exact_quote": "In an encoded frame, let $T$ be a set of raw sites owned by $P_\\circ$ and disjoint from every outer hole footprint. The proposed birth changes only the owner of $T$, from $P_\\circ$ to $Q_\\circ$. Keep every hole, its encoding, and its tag owner fixed. Define $$\\label{eq:birth-partition}\n E=\\{x:\\text{the old raw owner of }x\\ne P_\\circ\\}\n        \\cup H^+(\\mathcal F),\\qquad\n U=\\Lambda\\setminus(T\\cup E).$$ If $I_\\Omega(T:E)\\le L^{-60}$, the birth is a bounded change involving only $P_\\circ,Q_\\circ$, with reference-vector error at most $L^{-30}$. The reversed death obeys the same bound whenever these ownership and information conditions hold in its surrounding frame, namely the frame *after* the death.",
        "source_id": "PM-265.2"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-exchange",
        "exact_quote": "Consider two encoded frames, and a plane region $Y$. Suppose every hole’s entire outer square lies on one side of $\\partial Y$. Inside $Y$, exchange the two raw ownership assignments and the two lists of encoded holes, including their tag owners; outside $Y$, keep both frames unchanged. Let $P_\\circ$ be a party, and on the physical square put $$\\begin{aligned}\n Z&=\\{x:\\text{the two old raw owners of }x\n                         \\text{ are not both }P_\\circ\\}\n                  \\cup H^+(\\mathcal F_1)\\cup H^+(\\mathcal F_2),\n                 \\label{eq:exchange-Z}\\\\\n T&=Z\\cap Y,\\qquad E=Z\\setminus Y,\\qquad\n U=\\Lambda\\setminus Z.\\label{eq:exchange-partition}\n\\end{aligned}$$ If $I_\\Omega(T:E)\\le L^{-60}$, the exchange can be implemented by private contractions and register renaming, with reference-vector error at most $4L^{-30}$. It uses $P_\\circ$ and the owners of the registers and tags being renamed. Thus, when these parties form a bounded list, it is a bounded change in the sense of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:compression\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:compression\">10</a>.",
        "source_id": "PM-265.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-exchange",
          "exact_quote": "Consider two encoded frames, and a plane region $Y$. Suppose every hole’s entire outer square lies on one side of $\\partial Y$. It uses $P_\\circ$ and the owners of the registers and tags being renamed. Thus, when these parties form a bounded list, it is a bounded change in the sense of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:compression\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:compression\">10</a>.",
          "source_id": "PM-265.2"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-protocol",
        "exact_quote": "For all sufficiently large $L$, there is a protocol with the following properties. All constants in this statement are uniform in the Hamiltonian and $L$.*\n\n1.  *There are $O(L^2)$ parties, one for each square in a padded dyadic hierarchy and at most a fixed number of additional parties for each repainting of a square. Initialization is a pure product across parties. One party initially holds the main copy of $\\Omega$; all auxiliary copies are initialized privately at their designated parties.*\n\n2.  *The schedule has at most $C L^3$ changes. Each change is implemented by a fixed number of contraction gates involving at most a fixed number of parties and having the bipartite-resource expansions established in Lemmas <a href=\"#lem:small-rewrite\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:small-rewrite\">14</a>, <a href=\"#lem:birth\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:birth\">16</a>, and <a href=\"#lem:exchange\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:exchange\">17</a>. The expansions can be made accurate to any fixed inverse power of $L$ in operator norm where an approximation is needed. Each party participates in at most a fixed number of gates involving other parties over its entire lifetime.*\n\n3.  *Every linked gate involves parties at the same or adjacent dyadic scales. Parties at a gate have addresses at distance $O(n)$ from one another, where $n$ is either endpoint scale; additional parties have the scale and address of the square to whose repainting they belong.*\n\n4.  *There is a designated reference vector at every stage, formed from the main encoded frame and the auxiliary encoded frames. All its factors have norm at most one. Each change takes its preceding reference vector within $C L^{-20}$ in vector norm of its following reference vector. At the end, the reference is $$\\Omega\\otimes\\Xi,\n     \\qquad 1-C L^{3-60}\\le \\lVert \\Xi\\rVert\\le1,$$ where $\\Xi$ contains only registers to be discarded. Every original physical site is a raw register at its own unit-square party. No other party has a physical output, so every party’s physical output dimension is at most $q$.",
        "source_id": "PM-265.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-protocol",
          "exact_quote": "For all sufficiently large $L$, there is a protocol with the following properties.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-geometry-angular",
        "exact_quote": "There is a fixed $a_0>0$ such that every unmodified elementary birth or death above has changed region $R$ of diameter $O(n)$ and surrounding guide $f$ satisfying $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}\\bigl(y,\\overline{\\{f\\ne P_\\circ\\}}\\bigr)\n \\ge a_0\\min\\{n,d_{\\mathcal V}(y)\\},\n \\qquad y\\in\\overline R.\n \\label{eq:geometry-birth-clearance}$$ For an unmodified lens exchange, every point in the closure of the positions that are not common $C$ on its two before guides satisfies $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(y,\\partial Y)\n \\ge a_0\\min\\{n,d_{\\mathcal V}(y)\\}.\n \\label{eq:geometry-exchange-clearance}$$ Here and throughout this section distances are ambient sup distances.",
        "source_id": "PM-265.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-geometry-angular",
          "exact_quote": "There is a fixed $a_0>0$ such that every unmodified elementary birth or death above has changed region $R$ of diameter $O(n)$ and surrounding guide $f$ satisfying $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}\\bigl(y,\\overline{\\{f\\ne P_\\circ\\}}\\bigr)\n \\ge a_0\\min\\{n,d_{\\mathcal V}(y)\\},\n \\qquad y\\in\\overline R.\n \\label{eq:geometry-birth-clearance}$$ For an unmodified lens exchange, every point in the closure of the positions that are not common $C$ on its two before guides satisfies $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(y,\\partial Y)\n \\ge a_0\\min\\{n,d_{\\mathcal V}(y)\\}.\n \\label{eq:geometry-exchange-clearance}$$ Here and throughout this section distances are ambient sup distances.",
          "source_id": "PM-265.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "There is a fixed $a_0>0$ such that every unmodified elementary birth or death above has changed region $R$ of diameter $O(n)$ and surrounding guide $f$ satisfying $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}\\bigl(y,\\overline{\\{f\\ne P_\\circ\\}}\\bigr)\n \\ge a_0\\min\\{n,d_{\\mathcal V}(y)\\},\n \\qquad y\\in\\overline R.\n \\label{eq:geometry-birth-clearance}$$ For an unmodified lens exchange, every point in the closure of the positions that are not common $C$ on its two before guides satisfies $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(y,\\partial Y)\n \\ge a_0\\min\\{n,d_{\\mathcal V}(y)\\}.\n \\label{eq:geometry-exchange-clearance}$$ Here and throughout this section distances are ambient sup distances.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-columns",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\Omega$ be a unit vector on a tensor product of finite-dimensional spaces. If an operator $\\sigma$ satisfies $\\lVert \\sigma-|\\Omega\\rangle\\langle\\Omega|\\rVert_1\\le\\eta<1$, then some product-basis column $v=\\sigma|z\\rangle$ is nonzero and satisfies $$\\min_\\theta\\left\\|\\frac{v}{\\lVert v\\rVert}-e^{i\\theta}\\Omega\\right\\|\n \\le2\\eta.$$ Fixing this column in an operator tensor network leaves all its virtual dimensions unchanged.",
        "source_id": "PM-265.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-columns",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\Omega$ be a unit vector on a tensor product of finite-dimensional spaces.           "source_id": "PM-265.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $\\Omega$ be a unit vector on a tensor product of finite-dimensional spaces.       "explanation": "An operator close to a normalized pure-state projector contains a nonzero product-basis column whose normalized vector is close to the target. Selecting that column preserves virtual dimensions and does not require positivity or a lower bound on the selected amplitude.",

      "explanation": "Comparable-scale nearby party links admit horizontal-then-vertical routing with constant congestion. Reflection of the padded grid preserves physical outputs and the polynomial bond bound.",

        }

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Open square lattice and physical Hilbert space** ([source](#pm-h-3)): For an integer $L\ge2$, let $\Lambda_L=\{1,\ldots,L\}^2$, with the nearest-neighbor square-lattice edge set $E(\Lambda_L)$ and open boundary conditions. Put $$\mathcal H_{\Lambda_L}=\bigotimes_{v\in\Lambda_L}\mathbb C^q.$$
- **Projected entangled-pair state** ([source](#pm-h-3)): A PEPS on this grid is the contraction obtained by placing $\sum_{a=1}^{D_e}\|a\rangle\otimes\|a\rangle$ on each edge $e$ and applying at each vertex an arbitrary linear map $$A_v:\bigotimes_{e\ni v}\mathbb C^{D_e}\longrightarrow\mathbb C^q.$$
- **Maximum bond dimension** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Its maximum bond dimension is $\max_eD_e$.
- **Ambient enlargement and physical sampling** ([source](#sec:preliminaries)): For a plane set $T$, write $T^{+r}=\{x:\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}_\infty(x,T)\le r\}$; sampling always means intersection with the lattice, and physical sampling includes intersection with $\Lambda$.
- **Entropy, mutual information, and conditional mutual information** ([source](#sec:preliminaries)): For a normalized state $\omega$ and a region $A$, let $\rho_{\omega,A}$ be its marginal, and set $$\begin{aligned} S_\omega(A)&=-\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\rho_{\omega,A}\log\rho_{\omega,A},\\ I_\omega(A:C)&=S_\omega(A)+S_\omega(C)-S_\omega(AC),\\ I_\omega(A:C\mid B)&=S_\omega(AB)+S_\omega(BC) -S_\omega(B)-S_\omega(ABC). \end{aligned}$$
- **Root fidelity and relative entropy** ([source](#pm-h-10)): For normalized density operators define $F(\rho,\sigma)=\lVert \sqrt\rho\sqrt\sigma\rVert_1$ and $D(\rho\Vert\sigma)=\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\rho(\log\rho-\log\sigma)$, with the usual infinite value when the support condition fails.
- **Inside rank of a cylinder** ([source](#sec:patches)): For a subspace $S\subseteq\mathcal H_X$, the cylinder $S\otimes\mathcal H_{\Lambda\setminus X}$ has *inside rank* $\dim S$.
- **Uniformly polynomial quantity** ([source](#sec:compression)): A quantity is *uniformly polynomial* if it is at most $C L^a$, where $C,a$ are independent of the particular construction and of all its private Hilbert-space dimensions.
- **Party** ([source](#pm-h-18)): A party is a label for a collection of registers.
- **Private gate** ([source](#pm-h-18)): A *private* gate has one participating party.
- **Allowed gate monomial** ([source](#pm-h-18)): An allowed monomial in a gate expansion is a finite composition of local contractions, preparations of normalized vectors shared between two participating parties, and contractions by normalized bras shared between two participating parties.
- **Sheet** ([source](#pm-h-28)): A *sheet* has one raw register $\mathbb C^q$ at each $x\in\Lambda$ and a specified owner for each register.
- **Hole encoder contraction identity** ([source](#eq:encoder-contraction)): In particular, the encoding retains raw registers at every site, even inside the hole.
- **Encoded frame** ([source](#stmt-def-frame)): An encoded frame $\mathcal F$ consists of a raw ownership assignment on one sheet, a collection of holes $(c,h)$ with pairwise disjoint outer squares, and a specified party holding each hole’s tag. Its encoding $K_{\mathcal F}$ is the product of the maps <a href="#eq:hole-encoder" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:hole-encoder">[eq:hole-encoder]</a>, with a fixed ordering of the newly appended tags, and its reference vector is $$\label{eq:frame-vector} \Omega_{\mathcal F}=K_{\mathcal F}\Omega.$$ A frame without holes is a raw sheet. An ownership guide in the plane specifies such an assignment by sampling its labels at the physical sites.
- **Bounded change** ([source](#pm-h-28)): We shall call a change *bounded* if it uses a fixed number of parties and gates of the type admitted by Theorem <a href="#thm:compression" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:compression">10</a>.
- **Ownership guide** ([source](#pm-h-34)): A *guide* is a finite polygonal partition of the ambient plane, whose labels name the parties owning the raw registers at its sampled sites.
- **True vertex** ([source](#pm-h-34)): A *true vertex* of a guide is a point lying in the closures of at least three distinct actual labels.
- **Surrounding guide of birth or death** ([source](#pm-h-36)): For a birth, its *surrounding guide* is the guide before the insertion; for a death, it is the guide after the removal.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-peps-maximum-bond | Maximum PEPS bond dimension | ≤ | $CL^c$ | The prefactor and exponent depend only on the fixed physical parameters. |
| q-normalized-global-vector-error | Phase-minimized normalized global vector error | ≤ | $L^{-1}$ | Fixed local dimension, interaction bound, and full-system gap. |
| q-subsystem-boundary-entropy | Ground-state subsystem entropy | ≤ | $C_A\|\partial_\Lambda A\|$ | The same area-law constant is uniform over system sizes and Hamiltonians in the class. |
| q-cell-conditional-information-cost | Conditional information of a cell against a separated exterior | ≤ | $C_1(1+\log s)^K$ | Constants do not depend on exterior cell choices or the remote shape. |
| q-reset-buffer-width | Information-reset buffer width | = | $C_p(1+b+\log L)$ | The accuracy exponent is fixed before the large-size threshold. |
| q-reset-core-information | Mutual information of reset cores | ≤ | $L^{-p}$ | Only the original full-system gap is used. |
| q-angular-region-information | Information under tapered angular separation | ≤ | $L^{-p}$ | Clearance constants and the error power are fixed uniformly in the Hamiltonian. |
| q-adaptive-patch-projection-error | Adaptive projection error on the ground vector | ≤ | $L^{-p}$ | Constants depend on fixed physical parameters, patch scale, and accuracy. |
| q-adaptive-patch-total-inside-rank | Sum of adaptive cylinder inside ranks | ≤ | $C L^c$ | The bound does not control internal tensor complexity of the inside vectors. |
| q-effect-elimination-operator-error | Pair-effect elimination operator error | ≤ | $\frac{r}{\sqrt m}\sum_{\xi}\|c_{\xi}\|$ | Stack length is an integer; the original coefficient sum controls the error. |
| q-effect-elimination-expansion-size | Number of source-only monomials | ≤ | $K m^r$ | The absolute coefficient sum is not increased. |
| q-density-compression-absolute-error | Absolute trace-norm density compression error | ≤ | $L^{-p}$ | Uniform polynomial counts and output dimensions; no bound on private dimensions or source Schmidt ranks. |
| q-open-group-truncation-error | Whole-group tensor projection error | ≤ | $\frac{g}{\sqrt{k}}$ | Projection rank is bounded in each group, independently of open-wire dimensions. |
| q-small-rewrite-reference-error | Canonical small-patch rewrite reference error | ≤ | $(m+r_{\rm old})\varepsilon$ | Patch containment, untouched-hole avoidance, separate owner tests, and a bounded party list are required. |
| q-buffer-splitting-vector-error | Buffer-isometry product-purification error | ≤ | $\sqrt{2(1-e^{-b/2})}$ | The enlarged purifying dimensions are unrestricted. |
| q-selected-column-normalized-error | Normalized selected-column vector error | ≤ | $2\eta$ | A nonzero column exists without positivity or a lower bound on its target amplitude. |
| q-routed-grid-bond-product | Routed physical-edge virtual dimension | ≤ | $(C_1L^r)^\chi$ | The congestion and original polynomial link exponent are fixed uniformly. |

Suppose that $H$ has a unique normalized ground vector $\\Omega$, up to phase, with ground energy $E_0$, and $$\\label{eq:global-gap}\n H-E_0I\\ge\\Delta\\bigl(I-|\\Omega\\rangle\\langle\\Omega|\\bigr).$$ Then there is a nonzero PEPS $\\Phi$ on the same grid with maximum bond dimension at most $CL^c$ such that $$\\label{eq:target-error}\n \\min_{\\theta\\in\\mathbb R}\n \\left\\|\\frac{\\Phi}{\\lVert \\Phi\\rVert}-e^{i\\theta}\\Omega\\right\\|\n \\le L^{-1}.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-265.2"
        }
      ],

    {
      "computational_model": null,
      "effectivity": "ineffective",
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-compression",
          "exact_quote": "Consider a family of distributed constructions indexed by $L\\ge2$ with the following uniform properties.*\n\n1.  *The number of parties and gates is uniformly polynomial. The initial vector is a tensor product of normalized private vectors, one at each party, and every gate is a contraction.*\n\n2.  *There is a constant $b$ such that each nonprivate gate involves at most $b$ parties, and each party participates in at most $b$ nonprivate gates over the entire construction.*\n\n3.  *Each nonprivate gate is a sum of uniformly polynomially many allowed monomials with uniformly polynomially bounded scalar coefficients. There is a constant $r$ bounding the number of pair sources, pair effects, and polynomial-dimensional messages in each monomial. All constituent local maps are contractions.*\n\n4.  *At the end, the intended physical output space of each party has uniformly polynomial dimension. All other registers are traced out party by party.*\n\n*Write $\\rho$ for the resulting physical output density operator; it is positive and may have trace less than one. For every fixed $p>0$, there is an operator tensor network $\\sigma$ satisfying $$\\lVert \\sigma-\\rho\\rVert_1\\le L^{-p}.$$ It has one node per party, a bounded number of incident virtual links at each node, and uniformly polynomial link dimensions. Every link joins two parties which participate in a common nonprivate gate of the original construction. The physical legs at a node are the ket and bra legs of that party’s intended output. The constants in these bounds depend only on $p$ and the fixed uniform data in the assumptions, including $b,r$ and the polynomial bounds, and not on private dimensions or source Schmidt ranks.*\n\n*The same conclusion holds if the gate expansions in the third assumption are available to every fixed inverse-polynomial operator-norm accuracy, with uniform polynomial bounds at each fixed accuracy.",
          "source_id": "PM-265.2"
        }
      ],
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "id": "q-density-compression-absolute-error",
      "kind": "bound",
      "prefactor": null,
      "quantity": "Absolute trace-norm density compression error",
      "regime": {
        "ambient": "Subnormalized physical output of a distributed contraction circuit.",
        "hypotheses": [
          {
            "anchor": "stmt-thm-compression",
            "exact_quote": "There is a constant $b$ such that each nonprivate gate involves at most $b$ parties, and each party participates in at most $b$ nonprivate gates over the entire construction.",
            "source_id": "PM-265.2"
          },
          {
            "anchor": "stmt-thm-compression",
            "exact_quote": "Each nonprivate gate is a sum of uniformly polynomially many allowed monomials with uniformly polynomially bounded scalar coefficients. There is a constant $r$ bounding the number of pair sources, pair effects, and polynomial-dimensional messages in each monomial. All constituent local maps are contractions.",
            "source_id": "PM-265.2"

        "parameter_scope": "Uniform polynomial counts and output dimensions; no bound on private dimensions or source Schmidt ranks.",
        "quantifiers": [

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-import-uniform-area-law** [claim](#stmt-prop-area-law) / [proof](#pm-h-49): Specialize the companion entropy theorem to the original nearest-neighbor square Hamiltonian. -> Uniform entropy bound for every physical subsystem.
- **step-establish-purification-overlap** [claim](#stmt-lem-fidelity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-fidelity): Use exponential convexity for quantum affinity and polar decomposition for purification overlap. -> Fidelity-information inequality and an isometric purification comparison.
- **step-convert-companion-collars** [claim](#stmt-prop-coarse) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-coarse): Use the full physical domain as the prescribed cut, apply collar amplification, and convert graph neighborhoods to ambient enlargements. -> Uniform coarse mutual-information bound outside a sublinear ambient collar.
- **step-order-cell-tile-families** [claim](#stmt-lem-cell-cmi) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cell-cmi): Build recursively separated rectangular tiles, process the families in opposite orders, and use purity duality for the latter family. -> Polylogarithmic conditional-information cost of a cell.
- **step-localize-two-sided-filter-and-polar-factor** [claim](#stmt-lem-reset) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-reset): Obtain a replica-swap overlap, perform Gaussian filtering in both directions, localize on the physical buffer, and extend the polar factor to a unitary. -> Inverse-polynomial core information after a logarithmic-plus-information buffer.
- **step-reset-each-annular-layer** [claim](#stmt-prop-angular) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-angular): Process the target by dyadic distance layers, add boundedly many new cells at each layer, and reset information before proceeding. -> Uniform inverse-polynomial information between the target and exterior.
- **step-optimize-nested-patch-filters** [claim](#stmt-prop-patch) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-patch): Minimize a regularized ordered filter norm, prove descending stationarity, control energy with weighted row and column bounds, and expand by the first head before orthogonalizing projected images. -> Accurate orthogonal cylinder projection with polynomial total inside rank at variable radii.
- **step-replace-effects-with-source-stacks** [claim](#stmt-lem-effects) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-effects): Average cyclic insertion positions and give every prescribed effect occurrence a common garbage stack across branches. -> Source-only expansion with controlled operator error and unchanged absolute coefficient sum.
- **step-compress-density-by-centered-source-sampling** [claim](#stmt-thm-compression) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-compression): Eliminate effects, embed common source slots, expand errors by corrected positions, use lifetime participation to open only affected gates, and apply weighted Schatten bounds before choosing a sampling realization. -> Absolute trace-norm operator-network approximation with polynomial links on the original party graph.
- **step-bound-whole-open-leg-groups** [claim](#stmt-lem-group-tensor) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-group-tensor): Merge tensor vertices without residual self-contractions, bound nuclear norms by Hilbert–Schmidt norms, and telescope separate group projections. -> Dimension-independent grouped truncation and bounded product-term coefficients.
- **step-rewrite-compatible-small-patches** [claim](#stmt-lem-small-rewrite) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-small-rewrite): Compose decoding, ordered patch projections, and encoding; remove private bypass wires; approximate the remaining whole-group tensor; rescale to a contraction. -> Bounded ownership rewrite with polynomial allowed-resource expansion.
- **step-split-purifying-buffer** [claim](#stmt-lem-splitting) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-splitting): Choose product marginal purifications, enlarge their factors, and extend an overlap-maximizing isometry to the entire original buffer. -> Nearby product purification with unrestricted enlarged dimensions.
- **step-implement-homogeneous-birth-and-reverse-death** [claim](#stmt-lem-birth) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-birth): Privately split the buffer, apply the purification effect, reinsert the pair state with the new owner, and undo the isometry. -> Bounded homogeneous ownership change with controlled reference error.
- **step-exchange-whole-sheet-regions** [claim](#stmt-lem-exchange) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-exchange): Rename sheets and complete hole tags within the region, then apply ordered common-owner private swap corrections. -> Bounded two-sheet assignment exchange preserving the encoded reference approximately.
- **step-verify-fixed-band-clearances** [claim](#stmt-lem-geometry-angular) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-geometry-angular): Check strict transverse interval margins, prove conical separation near marks, and use compactness away from them over the finite pattern family. -> Uniform angular clearance for all unmodified elementary moves.
- **step-assemble-dyadic-protocol-with-bounded-lifetimes** [claim](#stmt-prop-protocol) / [proof](#pm-h-42): Repaint dyadic children using buffered auxiliary sheets, treat marks with small-patch covers, resize holes, and retire auxiliary memories before later levels. -> Site-party physical outputs, bounded lifetime linked participation, and a retained auxiliary norm close to unity.
- **step-extract-nonzero-ket-column** [claim](#stmt-lem-columns) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-columns): Compare squared column errors with target basis amplitudes, select a relative-error column, and normalize it. -> Nonzero approximate ket with unchanged network virtual dimensions.
- **step-route-and-reflect-party-links** [claim](#stmt-lem-routing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-routing): Route horizontally then vertically through dyadic anchors, separate scales by row and column valuations, and reflect the padded grid. -> Constant congestion on the physical square with physical coordinates preserved.
- **step-combine-errors-and-complete-original-grid-peps** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#pm-h-47): Track retained norm and telescoping contraction error, compress the density, select a ket column, route links, and absorb finitely many small sizes into the uniform prefactor. -> Original-grid polynomial-bond PEPS with the stated normalized global-vector error.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-global-peps-approximation**: The approximant is nonzero, uses the original open square grid, has polynomial maximum bond dimension, and approximates the normalized ground vector globally after phase choice.
- **invariant-adaptive-cylinder-constraint**: The patch projection has mutually orthogonal cylinder ranges at nested variable radii and polynomial total inside rank; its error is measured on the original ground vector.
- **invariant-bounded-lifetime-compression**: Compression controls absolute trace-norm error with polynomial virtual dimensions independently of private dimensions and source Schmidt ranks; each virtual link connects participants of an original nonprivate gate.
- **invariant-column-preserves-virtual-dimensions**: A sufficiently accurate operator approximation to a normalized pure-state projector has a nonzero product-basis column whose normalized vector approximates the state; selecting that column preserves every virtual dimension.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-use-efficient-ground-state-discovery**: Treat polynomial bond dimension as an efficient procedure for finding the ground state from the Hamiltonian. -> The distribution begins with the ground vector already available as a private state; its existence does not provide an efficient tensor-discovery procedure.
- **anti-use-efficient-contraction**: Infer efficient observable evaluation from the polynomial virtual dimensions. -> The representation bound does not establish contraction complexity for the constructed approximants.
- **anti-use-gapless-or-degenerate-extension**: Apply the uniform representation bound to a closing-gap family or a degenerate ground space. -> The fixed positive gap and unique ground vector needed by the spectral filtering and approximation argument are not established.
- **anti-use-fixed-boundary-patch-truncation**: Replace the adaptive patch constraint by an accurate truncation at one prescribed boundary. -> The polynomial bound concerns a sum of cylinder ranges with different nested supports, rather than a common-radius inside space.
- **anti-use-per-layer-incidence-only**: Apply distributed density compression when participation is bounded only within each hierarchy level. -> Expanding gates at corrected endpoints can incur a coefficient cost that grows with the number of levels.
- **anti-use-normalizing-small-retained-density**: Normalize an arbitrarily small circuit output and retain the same absolute compression error guarantee. -> Normalization can magnify the absolute error; density compression alone does not ensure retained norm close to one.
- **anti-use-unproved-parent-structure**: Treat the approximating PEPS as injective or as possessing a gapped parent Hamiltonian. -> Global vector approximation does not certify those additional structural properties.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main result is an existence theorem rather than a finite executable ground-state approximation procedure. The source states: “Preparing the initial vector is permitted because we seek a representation, rather than an algorithm for finding it.” The compression proof also selects a low-error sampling realization existentially. Finite supporting operations do not supply an oracle that decides the full representation guarantee for arbitrary Hamiltonians and unrestricted tensor entries.

| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The ground-state representation does not supply a procedure for discovering its tensors from a Hamiltonian, so it does not establish an executable ground-state solver. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The approximation concerns a static quantum ground vector. It supplies neither a time-evolution scheme nor a simulation update rule. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The intermediate representation permits a party to begin with the entire ground vector already prepared. This does not furnish a tool for obtaining that input. |
| algorithms | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | Given the party network, its dyadic addresses, and the locality and lifetime bounds, the proof provides a concrete routing construction that preserves physical output positions and bounded edge congestion. |
| data_structures | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | For an explicitly supplied finite vector, the small-size argument gives an exact tensor representation on a spanning tree. Its shared configuration index and equality tensors provide a concrete representation construction, without an efficiency guarantee. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Private work spaces and resource Schmidt ranks can be arbitrarily large, while sampled tensor entries need not be bounded. The virtual-dimension result therefore does not specify accelerator memory, numerical precision, or execution resources. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Density-weighted norm comparisons are related to privacy amplification and decoupling, but the paper constructs a tensor-network approximation rather than a cryptographic protocol or security guarantee. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The tensor entries may depend arbitrarily on the Hamiltonian and ground vector. The result gives no learning rule, training guarantee, or method for recovering those entries from observations. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The nested-filter argument minimizes over density operators and proves attainment, but supplies no optimizer with a convergence or computational cost guarantee. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The parties are abstract owners of quantum registers executing arbitrary linear contractions with unrestricted private dimensions. The bounded participation theorem does not provide a classical distributed deployment or a protocol for efficiently preparing its initial states. |
| other (Mathematical physics and quantum tensor-network representations) | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | The proof constructs a ground-state PEPS representation through adaptive encodings, a bounded-participation ownership protocol, density compression, column extraction, and routing. This is a structural existence construction with unrestricted tensor entries. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-fixed-uniform-physical-parameters** (hypothesis): The polynomial representation guarantee fixes the local dimension, interaction-strength bound, and positive full-system gap before varying the lattice size. A closing gap or increasing local parameters is outside the stated uniform regime. Blocked use: ['Claim a uniform polynomial bond bound for a family with a closing spectral gap.', 'Apply the same constants to unbounded interaction strengths or varying local dimensions.']
- **limit-unique-ground-vector** (hypothesis): The main representation theorem requires a unique normalized ground vector and the stated gap inequality relative to its projector. It does not establish the same conclusion for a degenerate ground space. Blocked use: ['Treat an arbitrary vector in a degenerate ground space as covered by the main theorem.']
- **limit-open-square-nearest-neighbor-geometry** (formal_scope): The original-grid representation theorem concerns an open square with bounded on-site and nearest-neighbor interactions. Periodic boundaries, other interaction graphs, and longer-range interactions require additional arguments. Blocked use: ['Transfer the original-grid bond bound directly to a torus or another lattice geometry.', 'Replace the designated nearest-neighbor terms by unrestricted long-range interactions.']
- **limit-stronger-companion-interface** (hypothesis): The representation proof uses the companion's collar and amplification interfaces and geometric ordering in addition to its entropy area law. Separate entropy bounds on cuts do not supply the compatible operations proved here. Blocked use: ['Infer the representation conclusion from a cutwise entropy inequality alone.', 'Apply the proof to a general state merely because its entropies satisfy an area law.']
- **limit-state-discovery-and-private-initialization** (nonconstructive): The protocol begins with the ground vector privately supplied and permits arbitrary private contractions. Its existence does not furnish an efficient method for discovering or preparing the ground state from the Hamiltonian. Blocked use: ['Present the distribution protocol as an efficient ground-state solver.', 'Count private preparation of the unknown ground vector as an available computational primitive.']
- **limit-contraction-and-entry-complexity** (formal_scope): Polynomial virtual dimension does not establish efficient contraction, controlled numerical precision, or a bound on the classical description complexity of the tensor entries. The cited general contraction obstruction does not classify these particular approximants. Blocked use: ['Infer efficient observable evaluation from the bond bound.', 'Assign practical numerical resource budgets from virtual dimensions alone.', 'Claim hardness for these particular approximants solely from general PEPS contraction obstructions.']
- **limit-no-parent-structure-guarantee** (formal_scope): The approximating PEPS is not required to be injective or to have a gapped parent Hamiltonian. These properties need separate proofs. Blocked use: ['Use the approximation theorem as an injectivity certificate.', 'Infer a parent-Hamiltonian gap from global vector closeness.']
- **limit-cell-conditioning-and-clearance** (hypothesis): The cell information estimate requires conditioning on a union of other cells and a remote set separated from the full ambient cell. Its conditional bound is proved through the specified family ordering, rather than by conditioning an ordinary mutual-information estimate. Blocked use: ['Use an arbitrary subset of nearby sites as the conditioning system without checking the cell-union hypothesis.', 'Measure separation only from occupied sites while ignoring the ambient cell.', 'Infer the conditional bound merely by adding conditioning to an ordinary information inequality.']
- **limit-reset-core-buffer-width** (hypothesis): Information reset requires disjoint regions, an initial information bound, and strict clearance of both cores from the respective region complements. The buffer width depends on both the information cost and the logarithm of lattice size. Blocked use: ['Reset information when only one core has the required buffer.', 'Replace the information-dependent width by a fixed buffer without proving the required inequality.']
- **limit-angular-floor-and-fixed-mark-count** (hypothesis): Angular separation retains a logarithmic minimum clearance, a fixed bound on the marked-point count, and the stated scale and diameter restrictions. Unmodified contact points do not by themselves satisfy this minimum-clearance condition. Blocked use: ['Apply the angular information estimate at vanishing clearance near contact points.', 'Let the marked-point count grow with lattice size while retaining the same constants.', 'Omit the scale and diameter restrictions.']
- **limit-adaptive-radii-and-inside-complexity** (approximation): The adaptive patch projection uses cylinder ranges at potentially different nested radii. Its polynomial total inside rank does not provide an accurate truncation at a prescribed common boundary or a bound on the internal tensor complexity of the inside vectors. Blocked use: ['Replace the radius-tagged projection by a single fixed-boundary low-rank truncation.', 'Treat the inside-rank sum as an efficient representation of each inside vector.']
- **limit-ordered-noncommuting-filter-construction** (hypothesis): Nested-filter conjugations and the first-head expansion retain their prescribed order. Stationarity gives commutation of a filter with its corresponding marginal, rather than commutation between different filters. Blocked use: ['Reorder optimized filters or inverse-filter factors.', 'Replace the ordered first-head expansion by an expansion that assumes all filters commute.']
- **limit-pair-effect-common-garbage-and-rescaling** (hypothesis): Pair-effect elimination requires normalized resources and a supplied allowed monomial expansion. Branch comparisons use the same garbage output. The resulting gate is rescaled before circuit telescoping to restore contractivity, and its stack length is chosen from the original coefficient sum. Blocked use: ['Compare branches with incompatible garbage spaces.', 'Treat the unrescaled approximate gate as a contraction.', 'Choose stack length from an expansion whose size already depends on that length.']
- **limit-compression-lifetime-and-output-assumptions** (hypothesis): Density compression requires polynomial circuit size, normalized private product initialization, contraction gates, bounded gate arity and lifetime participation, polynomial allowed expansions, and polynomial physical-output dimensions. Participation bounded separately at each level does not satisfy the lifetime requirement. Blocked use: ['Apply compression with participation growing over the full construction.', 'Omit contraction or resource-normalization conditions.', 'Leave intended physical-output dimensions unrestricted.']
- **limit-sampling-existence-and-operator-positivity** (nonconstructive): The compression proof selects a sampling realization from an expected-error bound. It supplies no efficient selection or contraction procedure, and the resulting operator network need not be positive or Hermitian. Blocked use: ['Treat arbitrary samples as certified low-error realizations.', 'Use the compressed operator as a positive density matrix without separate verification.', 'Infer efficient network construction from the existence proof.']
- **limit-absolute-error-and-retained-norm** (approximation): Compression controls the subnormalized output in absolute trace norm. The application separately proves retained norm close to that of the normalized target; arbitrary renormalization can amplify absolute error. Blocked use: ['Normalize an arbitrarily small circuit output while retaining the same absolute approximation guarantee.', 'Skip auxiliary-norm accounting before extracting a ket.']
- **limit-whole-group-tensor-cut** (hypothesis): The tensor estimate assumes normalized vertex tensors, no self-edges, and bipartitions into whole original open-leg groups. It does not bound arbitrary cuts splitting those groups. Blocked use: ['Apply the nuclear-norm bound to a bipartition cutting through an original group.', 'Leave internal self-contractions in a graph while invoking the stated estimate.']
- **limit-small-rewrite-coverage-and-owner-tests** (hypothesis): Small-patch rewriting requires avoidance of untouched hole footprints, coverage of every changed raw site and every affected outer footprint, separate old and new owner tests outside the respective inner holes, and a bounded participating-party list. Overlap is allowed, but the factors retain their prescribed order. Blocked use: ['Cover only affected inner holes while leaving their outer footprints untreated.', 'Use a combined old-and-new owner count instead of the separate tests.', 'Rename an unintercepted raw wire to another party.', 'Assume overlapping patch projectors commute.']
- **limit-birth-death-buffer-and-hole-support** (hypothesis): Homogeneous ownership changes require the target outside every outer hole footprint and include all outer hole sites in the exterior system. A reversed death checks the information and ownership conditions in the frame after the death. Blocked use: ['Run the buffer isometry across an encoding support.', 'Check a death only against the frame before removal.', 'Transfer an arbitrary region touching a third owner without the required information bound.']
- **limit-exchange-whole-holes-and-common-owner** (hypothesis): A sheet exchange moves entire hole encodings and their tags on one side of the exchange boundary. It uses $P_\\circ$ and the owners of the registers and tags being renamed. <a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove that the unique ground state of a uniformly gapped nearest-neighbor Hamiltonian on an $`L\times L`$ square lattice admits a projected entangled-pair state approximation with bond dimension polynomial in <i>L</i> and global vector error at most <i>L</i><sup>−1</sup> after normalization. Only the gap of the full Hamiltonian is assumed. The result is an existence theorem, with constants uniform over Hamiltonians of fixed local dimension, interaction strength, and gap.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

A projected entangled-pair state (PEPS) represents a many-body vector by local tensors joined along the edges of its interaction lattice. The central approximation question is whether ground states of gapped local Hamiltonians admit such representations with modest virtual dimensions. An entropy area law controls every bipartite cut, but a global vector approximation requires more: the descriptions at different cuts must be compatible, their errors must remain small through the entire lattice, and the final tensor network must use the prescribed edges.

We prove a uniform polynomial bound for square lattices with arbitrary bounded on-site and nearest-neighbor interactions. The only spectral assumption is a unique ground vector and a positive gap for the full Hamiltonian. We use the entropy area law and the collar estimates of (OpenAI 2026) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[RepoAreaLaw](#ref-RepoAreaLaw)], always with their full-system hypotheses. The new argument establishes the stronger representation conclusion.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Statement of the result

For an integer $L\ge2$, let $\Lambda_L=\{1,\ldots,L\}^2$, with the nearest-neighbor square-lattice edge set $E(\Lambda_L)$ and open boundary conditions. Put $$\mathcal H_{\Lambda_L}=\bigotimes_{v\in\Lambda_L}\mathbb C^q.$$ A PEPS on this grid is the contraction obtained by placing $\sum_{a=1}^{D_e}|a\rangle\otimes|a\rangle$ on each edge $e$ and applying at each vertex an arbitrary linear map $$A_v:\bigotimes_{e\ni v}\mathbb C^{D_e}\longrightarrow\mathbb C^q.$$ Its maximum bond dimension is $\max_eD_e$. The maps need not be isometries, and the resulting vector need not be normalized.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

<a id="eq:model"></a>
<a id="eq:global-gap"></a>
<a id="eq:target-error"></a>
**Theorem 1**. *Fix an integer $q\ge2$ and constants $J,\Delta>0$. There are constants $C,c>0$, depending only on $q,J,\Delta$, with the following property. For every $L\ge2$, consider any Hermitian Hamiltonian $$\label{eq:model}
 H=\sum_{v\in\Lambda_L}h_v+\sum_{e\in E(\Lambda_L)}h_e,
